## Controller rulings (pre-execution)

- Ruling: ledger lives in the plan's sprint dir (`.superpowers/sdd/platform-p9-20261003/progress.md`), not the skill's default workspace -- plan §0.6 names this dir and wants it committed (`git add -f`) -- cost if wrong: none (path only).
- Ruling: wave lanes run in parallel, one leased pool each -- plan's architecture; disjoint file ownership per wave (§2.2) removes the SDD "no parallel implementers" conflict hazard -- cost if wrong: merge-time textual conflicts, resolved by root.
- Ruling: every subagent (lane, reviewer, root integrator) is dispatched with model opus (Opus 5.5) -- owner directive -- cost if wrong: token cost only.
- Ruling: root work (merges, builds, real runs, prints) is done by root-integrator subagents in pool-2, one bounded step-group per dispatch -- controller must not build/run itself to preserve context -- cost if wrong: more dispatches.
- Ruling: E1 task 0 (P0-FIX) is dispatched alone first, reviewed, merged at R0-2; E1 is then resumed for its wave-1 tasks -- R0-2 needs exactly that SHA early -- cost if wrong: one extra dispatch.
- Ruling: OD-P9-1 hurdle = plan placeholder 8.0% net/yr levered at S2 until owner states one -- plan §0.3 G-B2 -- cost if wrong: G-B2 pass/fail reprinted against the real hurdle.
- Ruling: OD-P9-2 = defer OD-3 to the P9 freeze (DEC-3, plan recommendation); R0-13 reads nothing -- cost if wrong: none for ~2 sessions (plan §6).
- Ruling: OD-P9-4/5/6 (data buys, atx-db asks) cannot be executed by this run (money / atx-db off-limits); AL-DATA is gated and builds nothing unless data already landed -- plan §2.1 gate -- cost if wrong: P9-S has 2 families not up to 4.
- Ruling: OD-P9-7 decline VWAP; OD-P9-8 confirm platform-first pace; OD-P9-9 no mined campaign -- plan recommendations -- cost if wrong: none stated by plan.
- Ruling: OD-P9-3 (production leverage, L>2) deferred to after the v8 report; P9 keeps L <= 2.0 -- plan recommendation -- cost if wrong: P9-X waits / reruns under owner's rule.

- SQL-6 Ruling (OQ-3): a cache may select its SQLite index by file presence only where the consumer's output bytes are identical either way and the choice is written into the run receipt; anything else needs an explicit flag -- presence is hidden state unless recorded -- cost if wrong: one flag added later.
- SQL-7 Ruling (OQ-4, 5, 6, 10, 12): SQL2's base is SQL1's task-1 commit on top of the wave-2 base; the catalog never opens sealed or large payloads and records the SHA-256 the manifests declare (marked "declared", distinct from "verified"); the real-tree catalog ingest is a root-only bounded run; B2 exposes a ledger chain-head function (added to wave2-carry for B2); wave-2 pools: SQL1 fresh pool-23, SQL2 fresh pool-24 -- as designed; "declared" vs "verified" keeps the catalog from claiming a check it did not make -- cost if wrong: small rework.

- Ruling B2-R1 (extends B2-4): cancellation in 1-rho is conditioning, not a port defect; bit-matching numpy BLAS is not attainable. So: (a) the engine computes paired quantities from the difference series directly wherever that is algebraically the same statistic (stable form); (b) the Python-vs-engine tolerance for paired floats is 1e-12 / (1 - rho), and printed lines + decision counts must be byte-equal whenever 1 - rho >= 1e-6; (c) a pair with 1 - rho < 1e-6 is refused as a degenerate pair (SE / t / p printed "na"), same as the self-pair; (d) root measures rho for the X-5 / Y-F0 pairs on the pinned references before the identity run and logs it -- P9 paired tests compare a child book to its parent, which are highly correlated by construction, so the rule must be stated before any cell is read -- if a real P9 pair lands under 1e-6 its paired test is unavailable and the prereg must name a fallback.
- Ruling B2-R2: match Python: a constant candidate gets SR 0 by the same rule Python applies (read the Python at `8538eb0d`; same threshold, not a new epsilon); gtest added -- PBO is a decision input -- none.

- Ruling W3-EARLY-2 (amends W3-EARLY, on the owner's note): a wave-3 lane starts as soon as each hard dependency is merged in root OR is APPROVE with 0 Required at a named lane head; it merges root's head and the approved head(s) first (git merge, never rebase), merges root's then-current head again before its report, and does last any item that sits in a file a still-running lane is rewriting (handing it off as pending if that lane is not in root by then). Common part for these lanes: `r6-wave3-common.md` -- root merges serially and takes about an hour per lane; holding every lane behind the merge queue leaves the host idle -- a dependency that root's re-proof returns costs its dependants a re-merge and a re-test; more conflicts land on lanes; more disk and more waiting at the build gate.

- Ruling COV-13 (root's finding on eigen ranks 00-02; the reading rule is fixed here, before the diagnostic is run): (a) no `atx-cov-v1` parameter moves (COV-5). (b) Root runs a zero-trial diagnostic D-COV-EIG on the two D-COV stores, reading factor-covariance blocks, factor names and the structural flags only (no return, no `bias.csv` value): for ranks 00-02 of each recipe, the median over sessions of eigenvalue / median eigenvalue, and the median squared-loading share on (i) factors flagged structural, (ii) the industry block plus the country / market factor, (iii) the style factors, with the five largest loadings by name. Reading rule: a rank is "structurally null" iff its median squared-loading share on (i) + (ii) is at least .99 (a direction the regression constraints make unattainable or empty, so its bias statistic measures nothing a portfolio can hold); such a rank is excluded from one more pooled eigen row ("without structurally null ranks") printed beside the two rows already listed (all ranks; without eigen-00), and P9-V's registered predictions quote that row. A rank that fails the rule stays in every pooled row and is printed as a finding. (c) Required carry for COV-MV: `mv-aim-v1` never inverts the factor covariance F by itself: V = X F X' + D is applied through a form that is defined for a singular F (D > 0), and a synthetic test with an exactly rank-deficient F shows the target finite and equal to the dense solve; the rule's `mv_aim` summary also prints the share of the MV target's forecast factor variance that lies on eigen ranks 00-02 (print-only) -- a V-inverse tilt is where a near-null factor direction can do harm, and the pooled eigen figure is P9-V's prediction; both must be read the same way whatever D-COV-EIG shows -- if the three directions are real model error rather than structure, the exclusion row flatters the store; the all-ranks row stays printed beside it.

- Ruling B2-14 (the lane's question: may the follow-up use `git checkout --theirs`): NO. The refused command is not retried, and no wholesale substitute is used either: no `git checkout --theirs / --ours`, no `git restore`, no `git show <rev>:<path> > <path>`, no copy over the file, no rewrite of the whole file from the other side. The follow-up merges root's current head and resolves each conflict region by an ordinary edit of that region (the Edit tool), which is the merge work B2-12 assigned to the lane; the report lists every region with the side kept and why; if an edit is itself refused, the lane stops and reports. The re-reviewer checks the resolution region by region (for `research_cycle.py`: E2 made the file a shim, so B2's hunks are re-placed on the split files and none may be silently dropped) -- a conflict has to be resolved by someone for the lane to exist at all, and a region-level edit is reviewable and leaves `HEAD` and `git merge --abort` intact; the owner is told of both the refusal and this ruling -- if the owner reads "do not route around it" as forbidding any resolution in favour of root's side, this ruling oversteps: the merge is one commit on a lane branch, and `git reset` to `294612c3` undoes it.

- Ruling PERM-1 (what "do not route around" means for agents; the owner is told): a refused command is never retried as it was and never replaced by another command with the same effect that the refusal did not offer. Where the refusal's own message names an accepted form of the same action (here: a literal path instead of a variable in a deletion of the agent's own scratch), using exactly that form is following the guard, is allowed once, and is reported in the final message like a refusal. Anything else stops and is reported -- the guard objected to the form, not to deleting the agent's own named scratch (which the disk rule requires) -- if the owner wants every refusal to end the action, M2i's basetemp deletion was one step too far; nothing but the agent's own scratch was removed.

- Ruling OD3P-1 (OD3P's concern; what "absent, TRAIN weights carried" means, fixed before any history run): a dropped member is treated on every date exactly as the engine already treats a member with no data on that date: it adds nothing to the composite and its mass stays in its theme ("missing-or-unoriented-mass-stays-in-theme"); the pinned TRAIN weights, signs, theme blocks and the runner's verify are unchanged, so theme-erc-v1 and ic-shrink bases are accepted. A theme whose members are all dropped is treated as the engine treats a theme with no present member. Route: an opt-in list flag on the IC exe (`--absent-members <file>` with `--absent-members-sha256 <hex>`; capability `absent-members-v1`; flag absent = bytes unchanged), tested by the equivalence that defines it (a listed member with data gives the bytes of the same run with that member's data removed). OD3P now: the carry tool stops zeroing weights; `fit.carry.drop` makes the cycle pass the two flags on history roles only and refuse when the IC exe lacks the capability; OD3P writes the list-file contract (schema, keys, flag spelling) as `od3p-absent-contract.md`. The C++ goes to a lane on D3W's files (the IC runner; D3W's second round or a follow-up, the PM decides at D3W's hand-back) and must be merged and APPROVE at the wave-3 gate (EXE-FREEZE: the IC exe may not change after the re-pin); otherwise the clean-source line is printed "not read: no producer" and nothing else changes (the line decides nothing, PRE-31) -- "weight 0" silently also cuts the theme's mass and is refused by theme-erc books; reusing the missing-member rule adds no new semantics and is what the history eras already do to members without coverage -- the clean-source book keeps each theme's mass by spreading it over fewer members, so it is not "the same book minus twelve signals"; the print says so.
- Ruling ALCLOCK-1 (AL-CLOCK's question; fixed before any read): the `info_clock` block (name-days, fast and missing name-days, fast share, planned-turnover share on fast name-days) is printed by the NAV exe only under a print-only opt-in flag `--info-clock-print` (NavSpec `options.info_clock_print`), which loads `ea_days_since` and prints the block at whatever rates the book runs; it is not a rule flag; with it only `summary.json` changes, by that block; flag absent = bytes unchanged; the rule `info-clock-rate-v1` prints the block only when the flag is named too. P9-C and its reference name the flag alike (as PRE-7a), and so do the two books of the OD-3 turnover-share prediction; the wave-3 P9-B0 names it on the Y-F0 reference iff AL-CLOCK is merged and APPROVE then -- "turnover share up" compares two books, so both need the same C++ producer -- one more flag on the references; if the opt-in cannot be byte-neutral the block is cut from the reference and the prediction is printed "no producer".
- Ruling ALCLOCK-2 (`source_sample_end`): `info-clock-rate-v1` registers `source_sample_end` 2019 (Bowles et al., the published sample) and is FLAGGED under PRE-19 / PRE-31; P9-C stays registered (PRE-14 unchanged); the clean-source book applies no `info-clock-rate-v1`; OD3P's `rules_pending` moves it to `rules`. The mapping of the paper's information-release date to `ea_days_since` is stated in the registration as a fixed choice -- the published sample is the one a reader can check -- none.

Before the first row no fit exists, so the book is flat; P9-W's printed dSR includes that flat start and the prereg says so; no second, windowed statistic is added -- an honest walk-forward has nothing to hold before its first fit, and a second window would be a second look -- P9-W's dSR is biased against the walk-forward book by about one flat month in 48; it decides nothing (PRE-39).

- Ruling TEST-1 (the owner's instruction above; amends ROOT-1, every lane's "suites" step in r7/r8/r9-dispatch and the reviewers' "run yourself" lines; binding from this line): until the gate, every lane and reviewer runs TARGETED checks only: single-TU compiles (`atx-build.ps1 check <file>`) while shaping; at most one target-scoped Debug build of the lane's own test target (plus the exe its tests need) per round; the lane's named gtest filter, never the whole target-tests; the lane's own pytest files, never the whole `scripts/tests`, never `test_stage_chain.py` unless the lane edited the chain; Python teeth by file; at most ONE C++ teeth build per lane (every refusal disabled at once, as practiced). DEFERRED to the gate (GATE-1; root, once, on the gate head): the whole `scripts/tests` under seeds 0 and 1, `atx-engine/tools/test_stage_chain.py`, the whole gtest targets, every Release build, the tiny-world canaries, the golden at 1 and 4 workers, the flag-absent identity of the parents. ROOT-1a: at each merge root does the merge-affected targeted checks only (a target-scoped Debug build of the touched test targets; the merged lane's named filter and pytest files; the lane's flag-absent identity tool if it has one; SQL2-CLS); the whole suites run once at the gate; a red found there is attributed by merge commit (`git bisect` over the merge commits is allowed) and returned to its lane as a fix round before any cell.
